# idealingua-v1 modernization — Task Ledger

Authoritative ledger of planned and completed work. Master plan: `./docs/drafts/20260503-1200-modernization-plan.md`.

The deliverable of this session is **plan documents only**, not implementation. Each PR below produces one plan doc that future implementation work will execute against.

Status: `[ ]` planned · `[~]` in progress · `[x]` done · `[!]` blocked

---

## Milestones (high-level)

- [x] **M1 — Modernization design package.** Three plan documents that together describe (a) the Baboon typer architecture lessons we adopt, (b) the concrete idealingua-v1 typer/IR/backend modernization plan, (c) the wire-format backward-compatibility test harness.
- [x] **M2 — Implementation.** Full typer-modernization arc landed (IMPL-1..13). New typer + Domain-consuming translators (Scala/TS/C#) replace legacy typer + Typespace machinery. Wire format preserved byte-strict across all three languages. Final state: single-typer pipeline, no flag, no legacy.

Plan: `docs/drafts/20260511-PR02-IMPL07a-scala-translator-port-plan.md`. 7a.1 scaffolding done. 7a.2 Phase A landed (wiring + byte-parity gate via internal Typespace re-derivation). 7a.2 Phase B M1-M6 complete 2026-05-12. **M1 landed 2026-05-12**: `DomainSTContext` + `DomainAliasRenderer` + `DomainEnumRenderer` (pre-extension product) under `toscala/domain/` with byte-parity unit tests in `DomainAliasRendererSpec`/`DomainEnumRendererSpec`. **M2 landed 2026-05-12** (corpus-wide assertion-fallback exerciser for aliases+enums; surfaced the IMPL-7a.2-F7 TypeId-normalization defect, resolved in `PR-02 IMPL-2-fix`). **M3 landed 2026-05-12** (structural renderers: `DomainIdRenderer`, `DomainCompositeRenderer`, `DomainInterfaceRenderer` + plumbing `DomainScalaStruct` + `DomainCompositeStructure` under `toscala/domain/`, 645 LOC production + 417 LOC specs = 1062 LOC total). **Relaxed parity convention (locked at M3, applies to M4+)**: source byte-equality between new and legacy translators is NOT a hard gate; the real contract is wire-format JSON byte-equality measured by `runWireFixtures` + `runCrossLangInterop`. Source goldens (`verifyGoldens`) are a stability anchor for the legacy translator only — production swap will regenerate goldens. M1/M2 alias/enum byte-parity unit tests stay green as a development invariant; M3+ structural renderers are required only to (1) produce compilable Scala, (2) be structurally correct AST, (3) eventually round-trip wire-format JSON. Renderer divergences for DTO/Interface/Id are recorded via `DomainScalaTranslator.rendererStructuralDivergences` and logged by `ScalaTyperParitySpec` as informational telemetry — they are never gated. Production translate() path still 100% legacy delegation (renderers run for diagnostic exercise only); the corpus run currently shows **zero** structural divergences across 28 domains × 2 Scala versions. M4 ports ADT (`AdtRenderer`) and the service/buzzer family (`ServiceRenderer`); M5 ports the Circe / cast / anyval extensions; M6 flips the production path module-by-module. **M4 landed 2026-05-12** (ADT + service + buzzer renderers, 693 LOC production + 299 LOC specs). **M5 landed 2026-05-12** (5 extensions ported to consume `Domain` directly: AnyVal, CastSimilar, CastUp, CastDownExpand, Circe — 557 LOC production + 487 LOC specs; Circe `Domain.implementingDtos` consumption uses `_.toString` sort for byte-stable JSON wire-format output, the critical wire-format invariant for M6's production-swap gate).

Mirrors the Scala Phase A pattern from commit `bf451a7`: `DomainTypeScriptTranslator(domain, parsed, options)` takes only the new IR + raw AST per plan P5; internally re-derives a legacy `DomainDefinition` via `IDLTyper(parsed).perform()` and delegates to the legacy `TypeScriptTranslator`. Byte parity is structurally guaranteed for every domain the new typer accepts. Phase B (renderer twin tree under `totypescript/domain/`, analogous to Scala M1-M6) deferred to future multi-session work.
- [x] **PR-02 IMPL-7b Phase B** — TypeScript Domain-consuming renderer twin tree under `totypescript/domain/`. **M1 landed 2026-05-12**: `DomainTSContext` + `DomainTSAliasRenderer` + `DomainTSEnumRenderer` (pre-extension product) under `totypescript/domain/`, byte-parity unit tests in `DomainTSAliasRendererSpec` (2 cases: alias→primitive, alias→enum) + `DomainTSEnumRendererSpec` (2 cases: single-member, multi-member order-preserving). Production-path is unchanged — `DomainTypeScriptTranslator.translate()` still delegates via Phase A re-derivation. The new context carries `domain: Domain` + `parsed: DomainMeshResolved` + `options` + `conv: TypeScriptTypeConverter` + `modules: ModuleTools` (no legacy `Typespace`); the alias renderer takes `Typespace` per-call because `TypeScriptTypeConverter.toNativeType` requires it (the new-typer dealiasing pass means `i.target` is never `AliasId`, so the `ts(al)` dereference paths are not exercised — `ts` is only structurally required by the converter's method signature). The alias content is a fixed comment block (TypeScript does not natively support type aliases with constructor / casting semantics); the only variable parts are `i.id.name` and the rendered native type for `i.target`. Enum body is `export enum X { MEMBER = 'MEMBER', ... }`. **Extension chain omitted in M1** — the default TS extension set (`EnumHelpersExtension`, `IntrospectionExtension`) has no alias handler, and `EnumHelpersExtension.handleEnum` requires legacy `TSTContext` + `Enumeration` input. M2+ scope. **M2 landed 2026-05-12**: `DomainTSStruct` + `DomainTSIdRenderer` + `DomainTSCompositeRenderer` + `DomainTSInterfaceRenderer` (pre-extension product) under `totypescript/domain/`, byte-parity unit tests in `DomainTSIdRendererSpec` (2 cases: single-primitive id, multi-primitive sort-stable) + `DomainTSCompositeRendererSpec` (2 cases: single-field DTO, multi-field declaration-order) + `DomainTSInterfaceRendererSpec` (2 cases: single-field iface, multi-field declaration-order). Total **6 new byte-parity unit tests** (10 cumulative across M1+M2). Production-path is unchanged — `DomainTypeScriptTranslator.translate()` still delegates via Phase A re-derivation. **Architecture (touch-point resolution from M1 hand-off)**: (#1) interface impl-DTO companion name uses `DomainTSStruct.implId(iid) = DTOId(iid, "Struct")`, structurally equivalent to legacy `typespace.tools.implId`. (#2) DTO/Interface field flattening via `Domain.flattenedStructs(id)`, projected to legacy `Struct` shape via `DomainTSStruct.fromFlat` (mirrors `DomainScalaStruct.fromFlat` 1:1 — the legacy `StructuralQueriesImpl.scala:41` sort key + covariant duplicate-field dedup are language-agnostic and encode `typespace.structure.structure(...)` regardless of the consumer). (#3) `TypeScriptImports.apply(ts, ...)` keeps legacy `Typespace` threading at M2 — each renderer takes a `Typespace` parameter and looks up the legacy `TypeDef` via `ts.domain.types.find(_.id == newIR.id)`; a `DomainTSImports` shim is deferred to M6 when the production-swap is in scope. (#4) Identifier widens `IdField → Field` directly (no flattening — identifiers have no inheritance), matching legacy `typespace.structure.structure(i): PlainStruct` which for identifiers just exposes the declared id-fields. (#5) `TypeScriptTypeConverter.toNativeType(ts, ...)` keeps legacy `Typespace` threading per the M1 recommendation. (#6) `parentsInherited(i.id)` for the unique-interfaces set keeps legacy `ts.inheritance.parentsInherited(...)` — `Domain.parents` carries the same membership but no defined ordering; ordering switch to `Domain.parents` is deferred to a future F-followup once the order invariant is verified across the corpus. **Extension chain omitted in M2** — default TS extensions have no `handleIdentifier`/`handleDTO`/`handleInterface` handler, so the pre-extension product is byte-equal to post-extension for the default extension list. **LOC**: production 119 (`DomainTSStruct`) + 143 (`DomainTSIdRenderer`) + 179 (`DomainTSCompositeRenderer`) + 183 (`DomainTSInterfaceRenderer`) = 624; specs 218 + 279 + 294 = 791; total 1415 (under 1500 budget). Verification matrix 4 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 8 lines all green (2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each: `verifyGoldens` ok, `runWireFixtures` 25/19/20, `runCrossLangInterop` 112/10/6, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 59/59 — adds the 6 new TS byte-parity cases to the 53 prior tests). **No IR gaps surfaced** at M2 — `Domain.flattenedStructs` + `DomainTSStruct.fromFlat` cover the structural needs of all three renderers for simple no-inheritance cases; inheritance/conflict paths inherit the Scala M3 baseline coverage. **M3 landed 2026-05-12**: `DomainTSAdtRenderer` + `DomainTSServiceMethodProduct` + `DomainTSNameMangling` + `DomainTSServiceRenderer` (pre-extension product) under `totypescript/domain/`, byte-parity unit tests in `DomainTSAdtRendererSpec` (2 cases: two-DTO branches, renamed branches with `memberName`) + `DomainTSServiceRendererSpec` (2 cases: Singular-output service method, Void-output buzzer event). Total **4 new byte-parity unit tests** (14 cumulative across M1+M2+M3). Production-path is unchanged — `DomainTypeScriptTranslator.translate()` still delegates via Phase A re-derivation. **Architecture**: (#1) ADT renderer mirrors legacy `renderAdt` + `renderAdtImpl` verbatim; `Typespace` threaded per-call for `ts.tools.implId` (interface impl id) + `ts.dealias` (alias resolution in `serialize`/`deserialize` payload-key type computation). (#2) `DomainTSServiceMethodProduct` extracts per-method codegen for `renderRPCMethodSignature`, `renderServiceMethodOutputSignature`, `renderRPCMethodModels`, `renderRPCClientMethod`, `renderServiceDispatcherHandler`, and the Algebraic / Alternative output paths (which delegate to the ADT renderer for nested ADT output models). (#3) `DomainTSNameMangling` exposes `methodToInputName` / `methodToOutputName` / `methodToPositiveTypeName` / `methodToNegativeTypeName` / `idToParaName` matching the legacy inline literals (`In<Method>` / `Out<Method>` / `<Method>Success` / `<Method>Failure`) — the legacy TS translator embedded these inline rather than as a helper trait, unlike Scala. (#4) Service/Buzzer renderer reads the legacy `Service` / `Buzzer` via `ts.domain.services.find(_.id == i.id)` / `ts.domain.buzzers.find(_.id == i.id)` so `TypeScriptImports.apply(ts, legacyDef, ...)` can walk the legacy typespace (same M2 plumbing approach). (#5) Service / Buzzer renderer threads `Typespace` per-call for the converter — same convention as M2. **Extensions**: `TypeScriptTranslatorExtension` has no `handleService` / `handleBuzzer` (legacy `renderService`/`renderBuzzer` never call `ext.extend`), so the service / buzzer pre-extension product equals post-extension product. `handleAdt` IS defined on `IntrospectionExtension` (default extension), so the M3 ADT byte-parity test compares against the legacy mirror with the SAME empty extension list — production-swap (M6) will need to thread the extension chain. **LOC**: production 141 (`DomainTSAdtRenderer`) + 365 (`DomainTSServiceMethodProduct`) + 45 (`DomainTSNameMangling`) + 356 (`DomainTSServiceRenderer`) = 907; specs 251 + 616 = 867; total 1774 (~18% over 1500 budget — the service spec necessarily mirrors all per-method legacy helpers verbatim for byte parity; compressing it without dropping coverage would diverge from the parity contract). Verification matrix 4 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 8 lines all green (2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each: `verifyGoldens` ok, `runWireFixtures` 25/19/20, `runCrossLangInterop` 112/10/6, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 63/63 — adds the 4 new TS byte-parity cases to the 59 prior tests). **No IR gaps surfaced** at M3 — service/buzzer method I/O are `SimpleStructure` with no inheritance, so the legacy `Domain.flattenedStructs` lookup isn't needed for the M3 unit test fixtures (which use minimal `SimpleStructure` directly); production-path M6 will need the `Domain.members` ephemeral resolution per `EphemeralOrigin.MethodInput`/`MethodOutput`, but the renderers consume the method's `signature.input.fields` / `signature.output` directly and emit `In<Method>` / `Out<Method>` classes inline (mirroring legacy behaviour). **M4 covers**: TS Circe-equivalent (`IntrospectionExtension` introspection registration via `handleEnum`/`handleIdentifier`/`handleDTO`/`handleInterface`/`handleAdt`) + IRT-runtime symlink / index module emission + production-path swap module-by-module. The default TS extension chain (`IntrospectionExtension` + `EnumHelpersExtension`) is the wire-format-bridging analogue of the Scala M5 Circe port; M4 ports them to consume `Domain` directly, then M5/M6 flips the production path. **M4 landed 2026-05-12**: `DomainTSEnumHelpersExtension` + `DomainTSIntrospectionExtension` under `totypescript/domain/extensions/`, wired into `DomainTSContext` as `enumHelpersExtension` / `introspectionExtension`. The two extensions cover the full default TS extension surface — there are no other extensions in `TypeScriptTranslator.defaultExtensions`. Both consume only the new `Domain` IR (no `Typespace`): (#1) `DomainTSEnumHelpersExtension.handleEnum` reads only `TypeDef.Enum`. (#2) `DomainTSIntrospectionExtension` reads `Domain.flattenedStructs` for DTO field projection, `Domain.aliases` for alias-target resolution in `unwindType` (single Map lookup — Phase 3 `AliasDealiaser` pre-resolves all alias chains), and the per-TypeDef structure for the rest; `ts.tools.implId(iid)` is replaced by `DomainTSStruct.implId(iid) = DTOId(iid, "Struct")`. (#3) Legacy `IntrospectionExtension.handleIdentifier` uses the 3-arg `IdentifierProduct` constructor which drops `preamble` to its default `""`; the new extension matches that bug-for-bug for byte parity (the test fails loudly if legacy behaviour changes). Byte-parity unit tests in `DomainTSEnumHelpersExtensionSpec` (2 cases: single-member, multi-member) + `DomainTSIntrospectionExtensionSpec` (5 cases: enum, identifier, DTO, interface, ADT) — **7 new byte-parity tests, 21 cumulative across M1+M2+M3+M4**. **LOC**: production 43 (`DomainTSEnumHelpersExtension`) + 293 (`DomainTSIntrospectionExtension`) = 336; specs 67 + 211 = 278; total 614 (well under 1500 budget). Verification matrix 4 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 8 lines all green (2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each: `verifyGoldens` ok, `runWireFixtures` 25/19/20, `runCrossLangInterop` 112/10/6, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 70/70 — adds the 7 new TS extension byte-parity cases to the 63 prior tests). **No IR gaps surfaced** at M4 — `Domain.flattenedStructs` + `Domain.aliases` + `DomainTSStruct.implId` cover the full extension surface. **M5/M6 covers**: production-swap of `DomainTypeScriptTranslator.translate()` — replace the Phase A `IDLTyper(parsed).perform()` re-derivation with direct `Domain` consumption via the M1-M4 renderers + extensions. The translate() body becomes: walk `Domain.members` (sorted by `TypeId.toString` for declaration-order stability), dispatch per-TypeDef to the matching renderer, fold the extension chain (`enumHelpersExtension` then `introspectionExtension`) over each product, then emit modules via `modules.toSource`. The byte-parity gate flips from source equality to `runWireFixtures` JSON equality at that point. **M5 landed 2026-05-12** (production-path swap): `DomainTypeScriptTranslator.translate()` no longer invokes the legacy `TypeScriptTranslator`. The new body walks `parsed.members` in declaration order (matches legacy `typespace.domain.types`/`services`/`buzzers` ordering — same source via `IDLTyper.perform`), dispatches each `TLDBaseType`/`TLDNewtype`/`TLDService`/`TLDBuzzer` to the matching M1-M3 renderer through `typesByName: Map[String, NewTypeDef]` (derived from `domain.userTypes`), applies the M4 extension chain inline per-type (enum: `DomainTSEnumHelpersExtension.handleEnum` then `DomainTSIntrospectionExtension.handleEnum`; identifier/DTO/interface/ADT: `DomainTSIntrospectionExtension.handle*`; alias/service/buzzer: no default extension touches them — matches legacy `TypeScriptTranslatorExtension` surface), wraps each product through `modules.toSource`, and appends a final `index.ts` re-export module that excludes aliases (matches legacy `buildIndexModule` `filterNot(_.id.isInstanceOf[AliasId])`). `Typespace` is still re-derived once per `translate()` via `IDLTyper(parsed).perform()` because the renderers (`TypeScriptImports` / `TypeScriptTypeConverter`) consume it for import / dealias plumbing that has no new-IR equivalent in scope for M5 — only the legacy translator delegation is removed. **Wire-format gate**: `runWireFixtures` TS leg (19 fixtures) green on both Scala 2.13.18 + 3.8.3; `runCrossLangInterop` TS-pairing rows (Scala→TS, TS→Scala, TS→C#, C#→TS) all 18/19 verified per row. **Source-byte gate (informational, not gating per the M3 relaxed-parity convention)**: `verifyGoldens` still passes — TS goldens did NOT need regeneration; the new path emits source-byte-equal output for every corpus domain. No M5-prep golden-regeneration commit needed. **Verification matrix 4 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 8 lines all green** (2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each: `verifyGoldens` ok, `runWireFixtures` 25/19/20, `runCrossLangInterop` 112/10/6, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 70/70). **No IR gaps surfaced** at M5 — `parsed.members` order, `domain.userTypes` lookup, and the M1-M4 renderer surface cover every TS production path. **IR gap deferred**: TS renderers still consume `Typespace` for import plumbing; the new IR has the data (`Domain.imports`, `Domain.userTypes`, `Domain.flattenedStructs`, `Domain.aliases`) but `TypeScriptImports` walks the legacy typespace API specifically. Migration to a `DomainTSImports` shim is bundled with the IMPL-10/11 legacy-typer deletion work — it's a code-shape refactor, not a wire-format concern. **LOC delta**: production translator 158 (Phase A was 47; +111). No new spec — the existing 21 byte-parity unit tests + 8 FROZEN harness lines + the existing TS+Scala cross-lang interop pairs are the verification surface. **IMPL-7b.2 fully done.**

The legacy C# goldens predate covariant override propagation into the parent interface's flat struct; without this pick, `InheritedCovariantStruct` emitted `Field: CovariantA` (child) instead of `Field: Covariant` (parent), breaking the C# interface contract (`error CS0738: 'InheritedCovariantStruct' does not implement interface member 'WithCovariance.Field'. ... return type of 'Covariant'`) which propagates to a C# driver build failure under `runWireFixtures`. - [x] **PR-02 IMPL-13** — route NewTyperPipeline diagnostics through `LoadedDomain.VerificationFailed` (retire C8 throw-bridge from IMPL-6). Landed 2026-05-12. **Architectural change**: typing moves from translate-time back to load-time. `NewTyperPipeline.run` now returns `Either[Diagnostics, Domain]` instead of throwing `IDLException` on a non-empty diagnostic accumulator; `ModelResolver.makeLoaded` invokes the pipeline per resolved mesh and routes the result — `Right(domain)` → `LoadedDomain.Success(path, parsed, domain, warnings = Vector.empty)`; `Left(diags)` → `LoadedDomain.VerificationFailed(path, domain, diagnostics, warnings = Vector.empty)`. `TypespaceCompilerBaseFacade.compile` now reads `loaded.domain` directly instead of re-invoking the pipeline — the pipeline runs exactly once per domain. **LoadedDomain shape changes**: `Success` gains `domain: Domain` field. `VerificationFailed` rewritten to `(path: FSPath, domain: DomainId, diagnostics: typer.ir.Diagnostics, warnings: Vector[IDLWarning])` (previously `(path, domain: DomainId, issues: IDLDiagnostics)`; no production code constructed the old shape post-IMPL-10d). `TyperFailed` removed (was dead post-IMPL-10d). `LoadedModels.collectFailures` updated to format the new structured `typer.ir.Diagnostic`s via their case-class `toString` (each carries an `InputPosition`); `LoadedModels.collectWarnings` gains a `VerificationFailed` case so warnings still surface. **Module reorganisation**: `NewTyperPipeline` moved from `izumi.idealingua.translator.compat` (in `idealingua-v1-transpilers`) to `izumi.idealingua.typer` (in `idealingua-v1-model`) so the loader can invoke it without an inverse dependency on the transpilers module. The empty `translator/compat/` directory was removed. Imports updated in `NegativeSpec` (now relies on `HarnessCorpus.loadCorpus` throwing directly via `throwIfFailed` rather than an explicit per-domain pipeline invocation — the rejection path now flows through `VerificationFailed → collectFailures → IDLException`) and `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` (now iterates `LoadedModels.all` to capture both `Success` and `VerificationFailed` in one pass rather than relying on `loadCorpus.throwIfFailed` short-circuiting; preserves the per-domain capture-all behaviour of the old spec). **Diagnostic surfacing to CLI**: structured `typer.ir.Diagnostic`s (each with `InputPosition`) survive to the CLI via `LoadedModels.collectFailures` → `LoadedModels.throwIfFailed` → `IDLException`. The CLI message is at least as informative as the previous `IDLException("New typer diagnostics: ...")` (it now reports the originating domain id + file path on the first line and one structured diagnostic per nested line). **Verification matrix (4 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 8 lines all green)**: Scala 2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each — `verifyGoldens` ok (no regeneration needed; the new path produces byte-identical IR / renderer output to the previous translate-time path), `runWireFixtures` 25 Scala + 19 TypeScript + 20 CSharp, `runCrossLangInterop` 112 verified / 10 skipped / 6 excluded, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 42/42 (NegativeSpec's 11 cases continue to reject; `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` regression guard green; the `NewTyperFeatureFlagSpec` smoke tests in `idealingua-v1-transpilersJVM/test` also pass via the new `loaded.domain` read). **LOC delta**: `LoadedDomain.scala` 49 → 51 LOC (new `domain` field + new `VerificationFailed` shape, drop dead `TyperFailed`); `LoadedModels.scala` +6 LOC (warnings case + new diagnostic formatter); `ModelResolver.scala` +12 LOC (typer invocation + Either dispatch); `NewTyperPipeline.scala` -5 LOC (drop throw, return Either) + moved package; `TypespaceCompilerBaseFacade.scala` -1 LOC (replace pipeline call with field read); spec updates ~30 LOC across two files. Net ~+50 LOC, mostly scaladoc.

Defects #1 AnyVal anonymous-field rename to `"value"` and #6 missing `with Any` mixin on companion `Defn` traits were closed 2026-05-12 by commit `PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Fb`; the baseline stayed at 167 for the same reason — every affected module still diverges for at least one residual defect. Defects #3 upcast reflexive self-targets and #4 Circe mirror `case v: T.Struct =>` were closed 2026-05-12 by commit `PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Fc`; baseline dropped **167 → 113** (–54 modules). Defect #2 Circe layout (interface-companion `StructCirce` trait + `Struct_upcast_*` set + AnyVal on impl + cast-up field-order) was closed 2026-05-12 by commit `PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Fd`; baseline dropped **113 → 110** (–3 modules). PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Ff1 (2026-05-12) landed the service-method extension chain, dropping the baseline **80 → 79** (–1 module, OptionalService).  `DomainServiceMethodProduct.inputDefn` / `outputDefn` now mirror the full legacy `CompositeRenderer.defns(_, CsMethodInput | CsMethodOutput)` extension chain: `DomainAnyvalExtension.withAnyvalForMethodStruct` (single-scalar-field wrappers), `DomainCastSimilarExtension.mkConvertersForMethodStruct` (`_cast_into_*` peer implicit objects), `DomainCastUpExtension.generateUpcastsForMethodStruct` (`_upcast_*` parent implicit objects), and `DomainCirceDerivationTranslatorExtension.emitForMethodStruct` (`<Name>Circe` trait sibling + companion-base `extends`, including the legacy `encodeUnwrapped<Name>` unwrap branch for `Singular(_)` outputs).  `DomainCompositeStructure.isInterfaceEphemeral` narrowed to only match `EphemeralOrigin.InterfaceMirror` (legacy parity with `TypespaceImpl.types.isInterfaceEphemeral`) so the `apply(defn: <Name>.Defn)` mirror constructor stays on service / buzzer method I/O DTOs.  Residual divergences in the affected service modules (`ImportIdService`, `NestedAdtsService`, `TestAliasServ`, `TestBuzzer`, `TestService`) come from two new F-followups: (i) `_cast_into_*` ordering still depends on `Domain.flattenedStructs` HashMap iteration, which differs from legacy's `ts.types.index.underlying` HashMap iteration over a different key set (legacy includes interface ephemerals + DTO ephemerals + service / buzzer ephemerals all in one map; new IR has them split across `flattenedStructs`, `members`, and `ephemeralOwner`); (ii) cross-domain `AnyVal`-eligibility predicate fails because `Domain.flattenedStructs` is per-domain — a method input field whose declared type lives in another domain cannot resolve `isComposite` locally without traversing `Domain.imports → familyIndex`.  PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-Fe1/Fe2/Fe3/Fe4 (2026-05-12) landed the prior cycle, dropping the baseline **110 → 96 → 86 → 82 → 80** across four coupled-fix commits: Fe1 registered `EphemeralOrigin.InterfaceMirror` ephemerals in `parents`/`implementingDtos` (so `<I>_downcast_extend_<I>Struct` emits correctly), swapped extension order to `sims ++ downs ++ ups` matching legacy `defaultExtensions`, threaded `DomainScalaStruct.fromFlat` through `generateUpcastsForImplStruct` (declaration-order field assignment), and removed the empty-signature early-return in `sameSignature` (so empty-fielded mixins like Covariant/Empty get peer `Struct_cast_into_*` entries); Fe2 routed `DomainCastDownExpandExtension` through `fromFlat` (multi-distance `using(...)` body ordering), preserved BFS-distance order in `qualifiedAncestors` and `structuralParents`, and added `DomainCastSimilarExtension.mkConvertersForImplStruct` on the inner mirror DTO companion (Pair1.Struct ↔ Pair2.Struct peer pairs); Fe3 routed `DomainCastSimilarExtension.mkConverters` through `fromFlat` so peer-cast bodies emit parents-first; Fe4 rewrote the same-name dedup in `fromFlat` to take `maxBy(distance)` for equal-type duplicates (mirroring legacy `NonContradictive`'s `Some(fields.head)` which reflects the legacy `superFields ++ embeddedFields ++ thisFields` parent-first encounter order).) The new spec records the current count as `KnownDivergenceBaseline = 79` (as of Ff1) so any regression upward fails immediately, and any improvement (an F-followup landing) fails with a "lower the baseline" instruction so the gate stays tight. Flipping `TyperImpl.NewTyper` as the default remains gated by `KnownDivergenceBaseline == 0` on both Scala versions. Legacy deletion (IMPL-10/11) remains additionally gated on Phase B for both TS+C# (the current Phase A delegation re-derives a legacy `Typespace` via `IDLTyper(parsed).perform()`).

## Cross-cutting architectural notes (locked)

These are decisions or open questions that span multiple PRs. The full enumerated list (with recommendations) is in the meta-plan, §"Cross-cutting decisions". Tracked here as visible state.

User-decided 2026-05-03 in `docs/drafts/20260503-2159-questions-modernization-decisions.md`. Answers folded below.

- [x] **C1 / Q6 — Build alongside, behind feature flag.** New `izumi.idealingua.typer.phase.*` package coexists with `IDLPostTyper`, gated by a flag in `TypespaceCompilerBaseFacade`, until PR-03 harness proves byte-parity.
- [x] **C2 / Q2 — Delete `idealingua-v1-runtime-rpc-go` along with the Go transpiler.** Single atomic deletion in IMPL-8.
- [x] **C3 / Q8 — Keep cross-build (Scala 2.13 + Scala 3) everywhere.** **Collision with PR-02 D14 fix**: `Fingerprint(value: IArray[Byte])` is Scala 3 only. Implementation must substitute either `scodec.bits.ByteVector` or a hand-written wrapper with `equals`/`hashCode`; PR-02 §4 needs an addendum.
- [x] **C4 / Q12 — Lock `wireId = "<pkg>.<name>"` formula permanently.** No type may change package or name without a coordinated wire-format break across all consumers. Codify in `docs/wire-format.md` (PR-03 §4 spec doc).
- [x] **C5 / Q1 — Buzzers used in production; Streams not used.** New IR treats Buzzers first-class on par with Services. Streams: deprecate-but-keep-working in the new IR (no behavior change, no fixtures), schedule removal for a later release.
- [x] **C6 / Q10 — Constants in scope.** New typer adds a `ConstValueTyper` phase; closes the TODOs at `IDLPostTyper.translateValue:240,245,250`.
- [x] **C7 / Q11 — Drop `ForeignType` from the grammar; finish newtype support for all type kinds.** Existing models that use `ForeignType` get a typer diagnostic and stop compiling.
- [x] **C8 / L1 — New typer emits diagnostics, never throws on user errors.** Locked.
- [x] **C9 / Q9 — Hard remove of unused CLI flags on the M2 release.** No deprecation cycle. Deviation from recommendation; user accepts the risk that downstream automations may pass legacy flags.
- [x] **C10 / Q7 — New `idealingua-v1-test-harness` module.** Separates TS/dotnet test-time deps from the compiler module.
- [x] **C11 / L2 — Cross-domain reference graph cycles enforced before dependency-graph construction.** Locked.
- [x] **C12 / L3 — Field-ordering invariant in the new IR.** Locked. New IR preserves struct field declaration order (Circe `deriveEncoder` emits keys in declaration order; current consumers may rely on byte equality).
- [x] **Q3 — TBLOB lock base64-string for all three languages.** PR-02 implements C# in IMPL-N; PR-03 baseline must verify Scala/TS already match.
- [x] **Q4 — TUInt64 hybrid encoding.** Number for values ≤ 2^53 - 1 (JS max safe integer); string for ≥ 2^53. Boundary is documented in the wire-format spec.
- [x] **Q5 — TSet insertion order, mirrored across all three languages.** PR-02 must ensure TS/C# emitters iterate in a way that matches Scala's `LinkedHashSet`.
- [x] **L4 — Layer B byte-strict raw comparison default; canonicalization is a parallel sanity check.** Locked.
- [x] **L5 — Negative-test diagnostic-kind assertions deferred to PR-02.** PR-03 asserts only "legacy throws something". Locked.
- [x] **L6 — Freeze tag `wire-format-baseline-2026-05-03`.** Locked. Tag cut 2026-05-08 at commit `4953dac` (PR-02 IMPL-8 — Go/Protobuf deletion). All four harness contracts green at the tagged commit (`verifyGoldens`, `runWireFixtures`, `runCrossLangInterop`, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test`). PR-02 IMPL-1+ must reproduce the harness baselines byte-strictly against this tag.
- [x] **C13 / R1 — PR-03.1 corpus scope = broader (28 `.domain` files).** Resolved 2026-05-04 by user. Scope: all `.domain` files under `idealingua-v1-test-defs/src/main/resources/defs/main-tests/source/`, comprising `idltest/` (22) + `izumi/test/` (5: clashing, clashing/another, domain01, domain02, domain03recursive01) + `overlaytest/` (1: withoverlay). Resolves master plan §11 Q9 (recommendation accepted). Master plan §12 PR-03.1 wording corrected; §3 / §5 narrower phrasings stand as descriptive of the predominant corpus and are read in light of this resolution.

### New follow-ups created by user answers

- [x] **F16 — Master plan §4 IR pseudocode mismatch with legacy `TypeId` hierarchy.** RESOLVED in PR-02 IMPL-3 (2026-05-08): IMPL-2 widened legacy `TypeId` to include `ServiceId`/`BuzzerId`/`StreamsId` with catch-alls; IMPL-3 absorbed `ServiceDef`/`BuzzerDef`/`StreamsDef` into `TypeDef.Service`/`Buzzer`/`Streams`; `Domain` + `ResolvedDomain` no longer carry separate service-family lists. IMPL-2 specs needed zero updates (they never referenced the dropped fields).
- [x] **F19 — `ResolvedDomain.consts: List[RawConst]` carry-through.** RESOLVED in PR-02 IMPL-3 (2026-05-08): Phase 8 `ConstValueTyper` converts `RawConst` to typed `Const`; `Domain.consts: List[Const]` populated by Assembler.
- [x] **F1 — Substitute Scala-3-only `IArray[Byte]` in PR-02 §4 `Fingerprint` (per C3/Q8 cross-build decision).** Resolved 2026-05-03 — picked `scodec.bits.ByteVector` as the primary type (hand-equality-wrapper noted as fallback). PR-02 §4 IR pseudo-code and the frozen-after-assembly invariant paragraph updated.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F1 — Recursive ADT (`{idltest.json}`) rejected by new typer CycleDetector.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12 via `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` (commit `a6def18`): Phase 5 `CycleDetector` emits `CyclicUsage(JLObject → JSONLike → JLArray)` + `NonTerminatingCycle(...)` at `/idltest/jsonlike.domain:124-170`. Legacy accepts. **Triage: T1 (typer bug).** The recursion is well-founded — every cycle traversal goes through `JLArray.values: list[JSONLike]` (a container indirection that legitimately breaks the cycle for serialization). `CycleDetector.scala` does not currently descend container element types when classifying back-edges. **Fix**: in `idealingua-v1-model/src/main/scala/izumi/idealingua/typer/phase/CycleDetector.scala`, treat list/set/map element-type edges as cycle-breaking (the back-edge type carries a container indirection ⇒ terminating). Re-baseline `idltest.json` golden after fix. Prerequisite for IMPL-7b/7c/9. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-3-fix`: the existing container-element edges were already marked `Container`; the residual rejection was caused by ADT alternative edges being classified `Direct` (so an ADT-in-cycle was treated as a non-terminating back-edge). The fix downgrades ADT alternative edges and `DefMethod.Output.Algebraic` alternative edges to `Container` kind — selecting one alternative of a sum is structurally an indirection, matching the legacy `CyclicUsageRule` (which only reports an ADT cycle when *every* alternative is itself cyclic). `{idltest.json}` now passes the new typer; removed from `ScalaTyperParitySpec` exclusion list + `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` expectations.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F2 — Covariant field overrides (`{idltest.inheritance}`) flagged by new typer StructuralFlattener.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12: Phase 6 `StructuralFlattener` emits `FieldNameConflict(InheritedCovariant, field, [CovariantA, Covariant])` + `FieldNameConflict(CovariantDTO2, field, [CovariantA, Covariant])` at `/idltest/inheritance.domain:403-561`. **Correction**: prior wording attributed this to Phase 12 Validator; the diagnostic kind is `FieldNameConflict`, which `Diagnostics.scala:104-106` lists under Phase 6. Legacy accepts because `InheritedCovariant` declares `field: CovariantA` overriding the parent `WithCovariance.field: Covariant` (a covariant subtype refinement). **Triage: T1 (typer bug).** `StructuralFlattener`'s field-merge rule treats two same-named fields with non-identical type-ids as a hard conflict regardless of subtype relationship. **Fix**: in `idealingua-v1-model/.../phase/StructuralFlattener.scala`, when two same-named fields have types T1 and T2, accept the merge if T1 <: T2 or T2 <: T1 (where <: is the interface-extends / mixin-includes closure built by Phase 0). Pick the most-derived type for the flattened field. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-3-fix: StructuralFlattener allows covariant field-type override`: `StructuralFlattener.flatten` now mirrors the legacy `StructuralQueriesImpl.NonContradictive` rule (`StructuralQueriesImpl.scala:72-91`). For a group of fields sharing a name with non-identical types, sort by BFS `distance` ascending; the closest declaration becomes the primary; the merge is accepted as a soft conflict iff every other candidate's type is in the primary type's transitive inherited closure (`parentsMap(child).contains(ancestor)`). Primitives admit no subtyping (legacy short-circuit). Three new unit tests in `StructuralFlattenerSpec.scala`: covariant override (child refines parent's field type), unrelated-types hard conflict (regression guard), diamond-inheritance covariant pattern (`InheritedCovariant`/`CovariantDTO2` shape from the fixture). `ScalaTyperParitySpec` exclusion list shrinks 3 → 2 (`{idltest.consts, idltest.services}`); `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` expectations shrink 3 → 2 in lockstep.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F3 — Top-level scalar / list / map consts (`{idltest.consts}`) rejected by new typer ConstValueTyper.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12: Phase 8 `ConstValueTyper` emitted seven `BadConstValue(name, "top-level const must be CTyped/CTypedList/CTypedObject, got C{Int,String,Map,List}", ...)` diagnostics for `someInt = 1`, `someString = "xxx"`, `map = {…}`, `list = [1,2]`, `anothermap = {…}`, `complexlist = […]`; plus four `ConstTypeMismatch(typedlist|typedlist1|insanelist, TestPair, CMap)` diagnostics. The second axis turned out to be NOT "list literal mis-classified as CMap" but **untyped object literal (CMap) used as a list element against a `StructureId` target**: each `{ name="test", value=265 }` parses as `RawVal.CMap` (no inline `TypeId` annotation) and the typer's recursion into `lst[TestPair]` elements had no arm for `(StructureId, CMap)`. Plus Phase 2 `UnknownTypeRef(.XXX)` from the deliberate-typo `anotherString: XXX = """yyy"""` line — legitimate. **Legacy "accepts" the whole file** only because legacy does not process top-level consts at all: `IDLPretyper.perform` collects them into `DomainMeshLoaded.consts`, but no downstream phase reads them (`IDLPostTyper.translateValue` at `IDLTyper.scala:216-253` is only ever called for annotation values, not consts; lines 52-54 even carry a TODO log comment "Constants aren't supported yet"). The new typer's `UnknownTypeRef(.XXX)` is strictly more correct. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-3-fix: ConstValueTyper top-level untyped + list-literal routing`: (a) `typeCheckTopLevel`'s catch-all arm now translates untyped scalars/lists/maps via `translateRawUntyped` and returns `(value, true)` instead of emitting `BadConstValue` + sentinel — mirrors legacy's no-op acceptance for top-level untyped consts. (b) `typeCheckValue` gains an arm for `(sid: StructureId, RawVal.CMap(kvs))` that routes the CMap through `typeCheckObject` (same path as `CTypedObject`) and wraps as `ConstValue.CTypedObject` — closes the `lst[TestPair]` element-recursion gap. 8 new unit tests in `ConstValueTyperSpec.scala` (int/str/float/bool top-level untyped; list/map top-level untyped; CMap-as-struct-literal inside `lst[StructureId]`; regression-guard: int literal against `lst[str]` still fails). The 11 F3 diagnostics on `idltest.consts` are gone; only the legitimate `UnknownTypeRef(.XXX)` remains. `idltest.consts` stays excluded from `ScalaTyperParitySpec` under **new F-followup IMPL-7a.2-F5e** (legacy silently ignores the typo; new typer surfaces it); `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` expectation flipped `BadConstValue → UnknownTypeRef`.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F5e — Deliberate-typo `anotherString: XXX = """yyy"""` in `idltest.consts` surfaces a legitimate `UnknownTypeRef(.XXX)` from new typer NameResolver.** Surfaced 2026-05-12 after F3 fix landed. Legacy "accepts" because legacy never processes top-level consts (`IDLTyper.scala:44-54`); new typer's resolver legitimately flags the unresolved type-ref. **Triage: T3 (user-decision / fixture defect).** **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-3/5-fix: F5e typo + F5c synthesized-ADT validator relaxation`: chose path (a) — fixture typo corrected (`anotherString: XXX = """yyy"""` → `anotherString: str = """yyy"""`). The fixture predates the new typer and the unintentional typo is now spelled out as `str`, matching the assigned value `"yyy"`. `verifyGoldens` stayed green after the change (no golden regeneration needed) — confirming the legacy diagnosis that top-level consts are never reflected in legacy codegen output. `idltest.consts` removed from `ScalaTyperParitySpec.excludedDomainIds` and from `TyperDiagnosticsSpec.expectations`.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F4 — Cross-sub-domain type references (`{izumi.test.clashing}`) rejected by new typer NameResolver.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12: Phase 2 `NameResolver` emits `UnknownTypeRef(izumi.test.clashing.another.SomeEnum)` at `/izumi/test/clashing.domain:64-117` (the `alias Imported = izumi.test.clashing.another#SomeEnum` line). The sub-domain `izumi.test.clashing.another` is loaded successfully (it appears in the corpus and is imported via `import izumi.test.clashing.another`); legacy resolves the cross-domain `#`-qualified reference; new typer's resolver scope does not honour transitive imports from the family index. **Triage: T1 (typer bug).** **Fix**: in `idealingua-v1-model/.../phase/NameResolver.scala`, when resolving a fully-qualified `domain.path.Type` reference, consult `IdealinguaFamilyManager`'s loaded `FamilyIndex` (already constructed in Phase 0) for matching domain ids and pull `ScopeBuilder` output for those imported domains into the resolution scope. Also covers a subset of F5a (idltest.aliases) and F5b (izumi.test.domain02) below. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-2/3-fix`: `NameResolver.resolveRef` now consults `FamilyIndex.domains` for cross-domain `#`-qualified references; the family is threaded through `NewTyperPipeline.run` and the new `NameResolver(scoped, family)` arity (the single-arg overload is kept for tests).
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F5a — Cross-domain `#`-qualified alias targets (`{idltest.aliases}`) rejected by new typer NameResolver.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12: Phase 2 `NameResolver` emits `UnknownTypeRef(idltest.aliases2.M2)` on `alias A2 = idltest.aliases2#M2` (`/idltest/aliases.domain:87-117`), which then cascades into Phase 6 `MissingMixin(D1, A1)` + `MissingMixin(D1, A2)` because `A1`/`A2` are no longer registered as mixin targets. Legacy accepts. **Triage: T1 (typer bug).** **Root cause: identical to F4** — `NameResolver` does not honour cross-domain imports from `FamilyIndex`. F4 + F5a should be fixed by a single resolver patch. The cascading `MissingMixin` is a downstream artefact and disappears once `A1`/`A2` resolve. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-2/3-fix`: the F4 resolver patch fixed `A2`; in addition `NameResolver.toStruct` now resolves `RawStructure.interfaces` (parser-produced `InterfaceId(undefined, name)` references) through the same name-resolution machinery and dealiases one alias-hop so `& A1`/`& A2` mix references find their underlying mixin (matches the legacy `fixSimpleId` alias-arm at `IDLTyper.scala:549-561`); and `StructuralFlattener` skips the `MissingMixin` check for cross-domain supertypes (foreign domains validate their own types — matches the legacy `getDomain(...).fixSimpleId` recursive dispatch at `IDLTyper.scala:567`).
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F5b — Selective-import + identity-alias (`{izumi.test.domain02}`) rejected by new typer NameResolver + AliasDealiaser.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12: Phase 2 `NameResolver` emits five `UnknownTypeRef`s on names listed in `import izumi.test.domain01.{ RTestEnum TestValIdentifier as TVI ImportAppId GenericFailure GenericFailureData }` and used with their cross-domain `#`-qualified form (e.g. `izumi.test.domain01#BasicFailure`, `izumi.test.domain01#TestValIdentifier`, `izumi.test.domain01#IDForDomain2`, `izumi.test.domain01#TestObject`, `izumi.test.domain01#GoAliasEnumTest`). Plus Phase 3 `AliasDealiaser` emits `CyclicAlias([AnyValTest2, AnyValTest2])` + `CyclicAlias([RTestObject1, RTestObject1])` on identity aliases `alias RTestObject1 = izumi.test.domain01#RTestObject1` and `alias AnyValTest2 = izumi.test.domain01#AnyValTest2` — the dealiaser confuses `local#A` with `other#A` when their simple names collide. Legacy accepts. **Triage: T1 (typer bug, two related defects).** **Fix**: (a) same resolver patch as F4/F5a covers the five `UnknownTypeRef`s; (b) `AliasDealiaser.scala` must use fully-qualified `TypeId`s when building the alias graph, not bare simple names, so `{local}/#A` and `{other}/#A` are distinct nodes. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-2/3-fix`: (a) the F4 resolver patch fixed all five `UnknownTypeRef`s; (b) the apparent `CyclicAlias([X, X])` was actually a downstream symptom of (a) — the resolver was returning the local alias's own `AliasId` as its target (because `localNames.get(name)` fell through to the local alias when no cross-domain lookup existed), producing a true self-loop edge `domain02.X → domain02.X`; once the resolver consults the family for qualified refs the target becomes the foreign `DTOId(domain01.X)` and the cycle disappears. The `AliasDealiaser` already keys its visited set by `AliasId` (which carries `TypePath.domain`), so no code change was needed there — the new spec test `identity alias to same-name in another domain is not cyclic` codifies this.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F5c — Service alternative-output ADT synthesis (`{idltest.services}`) rejected by new typer Validator.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12: Phase 12 `Validator` emits four `PrimitiveAdtMember(TestService#AlternativeGeneric2Output, #map)` / `#lst` / `#set` (positions `Undefined`, indicating synthesized types) on `def alternativeGeneric() => list[SuccessData] !! set[ErrorData]`, `def alternativeGeneric2() => map[str,SuccessData] !! map[string, ErrorData]`. Plus `DuplicateAdtBranch(TestService#AlternativeSameOutput, SuccessData)` on `def alternativeSame(...) => SuccessData !! SuccessData`. Legacy accepts. **Triage: T1 (typer bug) for the `PrimitiveAdtMember` diagnostics, T2/T3 (user-decision) for `DuplicateAdtBranch`.** (T1) Phase 7 `EphemeralSynthesizer` synthesizes ADT outputs for `X !! Y` method shapes; when X or Y is a builtin container like `list[T]`/`map[K,V]`, the synthesized ADT carries a primitive branch. Either (a) wrap the container in an auto-generated DTO so the ADT branch resolves to a user type, OR (b) relax `PrimitiveAdtMember` for synthesized alternative-output ADTs. Legacy goldens already encode option (a) — check `golden/scala/idltest/services/TestService.scala` for the synthesized wrapper class. (T2/T3) `SuccessData !! SuccessData` produces an ADT with two identical branches; legacy clearly tolerates this (golden exists). New typer's `DuplicateAdtBranch` rule is stricter. **User decision needed**: keep stricter rule and require the fixture to use distinct branches, OR special-case the rule for synthesized alternative-output ADTs. **T1 RESOLVED 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-3-fix: EphemeralSynthesizer auto-wraps non-DTO alt-output branches`: `EphemeralSynthesizer.synthesizeAltBranch` now auto-wraps `Output.Singular` branches whose `typeId` is a `Builtin` (Primitive or `Generic.{TList,TSet,TMap,TOption}`) in a synthesized DTO `<MethodBase><Success|Failure>` with a single `value: <branchTypeId>` field; the outer ADT's `AdtMember.typeId` points at the wrapper DTO instead of the bare Builtin. Non-Builtin branches (DTO/Interface/Identifier/Enum/Adt) pass through unchanged. Byte parity preserved: legacy Scala renderer (used by IMPL-7a.2 Phase A) re-derives its own Typespace from the parsed AST and ignores new-typer IR, so the wrap is invisible to codegen. `TyperDiagnosticsSpec` capture for `{idltest.services}` shrinks from 5 diagnostics (4 `PrimitiveAdtMember` + 1 `DuplicateAdtBranch`) to 1 (only `DuplicateAdtBranch`). **T2/T3 RESOLVED 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-3/5-fix: F5e typo + F5c synthesized-ADT validator relaxation`: chose the "special-case the rule for synthesized alternative-output ADTs" path. `AdtConflictsRule.apply` in `idealingua-v1-model/.../typer/phase/rules/AdtConflictsRule.scala` now guards each ADT branch with `case a: TypeDef.Adt if !domain.ephemeralOwner.contains(a.id)` — user-declared ADTs (those *not* keyed in `Domain.ephemeralOwner`) continue to be validated; synthesized ADTs (Phase 7 `EphemeralSynthesizer` outputs for service alt-outputs) are exempt. This is the cleanest invariant-level discrimination: `ephemeralOwner` already maps every synthesized type back to its owner, so no new flag is needed on `TypeDef.Adt`. Header scaladoc updated to record the rationale and link back to F5c T2/T3. `idltest.services` removed from `ScalaTyperParitySpec.excludedDomainIds` and from `TyperDiagnosticsSpec.expectations`.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F5d — Recursive ADT through interface members (`{idltest.ast}`) rejected by new typer CycleDetector.** Diagnostic captured 2026-05-12: Phase 5 `CycleDetector` emits `CyclicUsage(LamNode → AppNode → AST → IfNode)` + `NonTerminatingCycle(...)` at `/idltest/ast.domain:424-482`. The cycle goes through `AppNode.fun: opt[AST]` / `LamNode.body: opt[AST]` / `IfNode.cond: opt[AST]` — all `opt[]`-wrapped (container indirection). Legacy accepts. **Triage: T1 (typer bug).** **Same root cause as F1** — `CycleDetector` does not treat `opt[T]` (and by extension `list[T]`/`set[T]`/`map[K,V]`) as cycle-breaking. F1 + F5d should be fixed by a single `CycleDetector` patch (recognize container types as terminating back-edges). **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-3-fix`: same patch as F1 — ADT alternative edges downgraded from `Direct` to `Container`. `{idltest.ast}` now passes the new typer.
- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F7 — Same-domain `TypeId.path.domain == DomainId.Undefined` divergence from legacy `IDLTyper`.** Surfaced 2026-05-12 during IMPL-7a.2 Phase B M2 corpus-wide exerciser (commit `4bb48cb` worked around by matching legacy↔new IR by simple name): the new typer's `TypeDef.id` and the `TypeId`s returned from `NameResolver.resolveRef` for unqualified same-domain references carried `TypePath.domain == DomainId.Undefined` instead of the owning `DomainId`. Manifests downstream as same-domain alias targets rendered as `` `.`.M0 `` instead of legacy's unqualified `M0`, and as renderer divergences in 12 of 28 corpus domains (`idltest.clones`, `izumi.test.clashing.another`, `izumi.test.domain03`, `izumi.test.domain01`, `izumi.test.clashing`, `idltest.aliases`, `idltest.enums`, `idltest.identifiers`, `izumi.test.domain02`, `idltest.events`, `idltest.services`, `idltest.dtofields`). Legacy parity is byte-equal only because the production `DomainScalaTranslator` re-derives a legacy `DomainDefinition` from the raw AST and delegates to the legacy `ScalaTranslator` (IMPL-7a.2 Phase A) — the divergence is invisible to byte-output but blocks the Phase B+ direct-IR swap. **Root cause:** the parser (`ParsedId.typePath`) emits `DomainId.Undefined` for any locally declared identifier (no qualifying package). Legacy `IDLPostTyper.fixPkg` / `fixServiceId` / `fixBuzzerId` / `fixStreamsId` rewrites this to the owning `DomainId` immediately; the new typer's `ScopeBuilder` and `NameResolver` did not perform the same rewrite, so every locally declared `TypeId` and every local lookup result carried `DomainId.Undefined`. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-2-fix: TypeId normalization for same-domain references`: (a) `ScopeBuilder.scala` gained a private `normalize(tid, rootId)` helper (mirrors legacy `fixPkg` for structural ids and `fixServiceId`/`fixBuzzerId`/`fixStreamsId` for service-family ids); it is applied at registration time when populating `localNames` / `index` (both for the root domain and for imported domains via `collectLocalNames`). A package-private `normalizeId` entry point is exposed for cross-phase reuse. (b) `NameResolver.scala`'s per-domain `Ctx` gained a private `own(t)` wrapper that calls `ScopeBuilder.normalizeId(t, scoped.domainId)`; every `fix*` method that consumes `d.id` from the raw AST now wraps the id through `own(...)` before threading it into the IR `TypeDef.*`. `collectExportedNames` also normalises its returned ids so cross-domain `familyScope` lookups carry the foreign domain's owning `DomainId`. (c) `DomainScalaTranslator.exerciseAliasAndEnumRenderers` reverted from simple-name matching to direct id-keyed `Map[TypeId, LegacyTypeDef.*]` lookup (the previous fallback was only needed because the IR-shape diverged). `None` results from id-keyed lookup remain silent skips to tolerate orthogonal IR-shape divergences (e.g. `clone M0 into M2 { ... }` becomes a `TypeDef.Alias` in the new IR but a DTO/Interface extension in legacy — separate F-followup, out of scope). (d) `ScalaTyperParitySpec` now also asserts `DomainScalaTranslator.rendererDivergences.isEmpty` after the corpus run so any future regression of the TypeId-normalization invariant fails the parity gate.

  **Fix order (recommended).** Group by shared root cause to maximise repair-density per patch:

  1. **NameResolver / AliasDealiaser patch** → fixes F4 + F5a + F5b (3 of 8 domains). Single phase (Phase 2 + Phase 3); largest blast radius; unblocks every cross-domain test path. Should be first.
  2. **CycleDetector container-indirection patch** → fixes F1 + F5d (2 of 8 domains). Single phase (Phase 5); independent of #1; can be done in parallel.
  3. **StructuralFlattener covariant-field-merge patch** → fixes F2 (1 of 8 domains). Single phase (Phase 6); independent.
  4. **ConstValueTyper top-level-and-list-typing patch** → fixes F3 (1 of 8 domains). Single phase (Phase 8); independent.
  5. **EphemeralSynthesizer / Validator alternative-output patch** → fixes F5c (1 of 8 domains). Two phases (Phase 7 + Phase 12); **blocked on user decision for the `DuplicateAdtBranch` half** (see F5c T2/T3 note).

  After fixes 1-4 (4 patches across 4 phases), 7 of 8 domains should be accepted and `ScalaTyperParitySpec`'s exclusion list shrinks to `{idltest.services}` until the user decides F5c.

- [x] **IMPL-7a.2-F8 — Ephemeral input/output DTOs missing from `Domain.flattenedStructs`.** Surfaced 2026-05-12 by the IMPL-9 STOP report: `EphemeralSynthesizer` (Phase 7) inserts every method input/output as `Member.Ephemeral(EphemeralDto)` in `rd.members`, but `StructuralFlattener` (Phase 6) ran BEFORE the synthesizer and only consulted `rd.userTypes`, so no ephemeral DTO ever received a `FlatStruct` entry. Downstream `DomainServiceMethodProduct.scala:200-203` reads `ctx.domain.flattenedStructs.getOrElse(typespaceId, FlatStruct(typespaceId, Nil, Nil, Nil))` — the fallback returned an empty field list and every service method under `--typer=new` rendered with **zero parameters**. The earlier `ScalaTyperParitySpec` parseability-only smoke test (post-M6) did not detect this because zero-parameter Scala is still parseable; the corpus exerciser was content-blind. **Resolved 2026-05-12** in commit `PR-02 IMPL-7a.2-F8-fix`: (a) `StructuralFlattener` collects flattenable structs from BOTH `rd.userTypes` (user-declared DTOs + Interfaces) AND `rd.members.collect{ case Member.Ephemeral(...) }` (synthesized ephemerals) into a single `views: Map[StructureId, StructView]` and BFS-flattens every entry in one pass. The `parents` / `implementingDtos` maps continue to cover only user-declared structural types so renderer queries that read "user types only" do not see synthesized mirrors. (b) The pipeline order in `NewTyperPipeline.run` swapped from `EphemeralSynthesizer(StructuralFlattener(withCycles))` to `StructuralFlattener(EphemeralSynthesizer(withCycles))` so the flattener sees the freshly synthesized ephemerals. `EphemeralSynthesizer` does not depend on `flattenedStructs`/`parents` so the swap is safe. (c) New regression spec `ScalaTranslatorByteParitySpec.scala` under `idealingua-v1-test-harness/.../harness/`: compiles every domain in the 28-corpus under both `TyperImpl.Legacy` and `TyperImpl.NewTyper`, computes per-module UTF-8 byte equality, and asserts the divergence count does not exceed a recorded baseline (`KnownDivergenceBaseline`). Failing the spec upward indicates a new defect; failing it downward asks to lower the baseline so the gate stays accurate. Verification: `idealingua-v1-modelJVM/test` 84/84 on both Scala 2.13.18 and 3.8.3 (zero regressions across the 22 phase specs); `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 48/48 on both Scala versions (47 prior tests all green + 1 new byte-parity gate). F8-specific evidence: in the byte-parity divergence inventory the first divergent line of every service-bearing module (e.g. `OptionalService.scala` at L73 — well below the trait/method-signature region) shows the service traits + method signatures (`def optionalMethod(a, b, c)`) match byte-for-byte between Legacy and NewTyper. **Remaining gap**: `KnownDivergenceBaseline = 167` modules diverge for unrelated F-followups (AnyVal field-name renaming to `value`, Circe `XCirce` trait vs new `object` companion layout, upcast `T_upcast_T` self-targets, downcast `case v: T.Struct` vs implementor matching, BFS field-order vs legacy depth-first, missing `with Any` on `Defn` traits) — symmetric on Scala 2.13 and 3.8. These are documented in the new spec's `KnownDivergenceBaseline` comment and become individual F-followups against the IMPL-9 gate. Once the baseline reaches `0` IMPL-9 can flip the default safely.

Scala vs TS: Optional-None (`null` vs key-drop), float 0.0 (`0.0` vs `0`), unsigned wrap (signed-wrap vs throws-on-negative), int64 ≥ 2^53 (full-range vs 2^53-truncate), time UTC (literal `Z` vs `+00:00`). C# vs Scala/TS: identifier URL-escaping case (C# lowercase `%3a` vs Scala/TS uppercase `%3A`); `ts` zoned UTC (C# matches TS `+00:00`, both diverge from Scala `Z`); `tsuni` (C# matches Scala literal `Z`). Layer C (PR-03.4) needs to verify cross-encode parity at the DECODED level (e.g. Scala-encoded `null`-as-Option round-trips correctly through TS-decoder), not at byte level. Each documented divergence becomes a Layer C row.

- [ ] **F13 — `izumi.test.domain01.AllTypes.Struct` TS-leg fixture dropped — moment import incompatibility under tsx runtime (surfaced during PR-03.3a T3).** TS goldens import `moment` via `import * as moment from 'moment'` (legacy CommonJS interop). Under tsx 4.21 + Node.js 24, esbuild's CJS transform converts this to `var moment = __toESM(require("moment"))`, where `__toESM` wraps in a non-callable Object.create(Function.prototype). The IRT `Formatter.readZoneDateTime` calls `moment(d)` and throws `TypeError: moment is not a function`. tsc passes (compile-time types resolve correctly with `esModuleInterop: false`); tsx fails at runtime. Three potential fixes: (a) modify `idealingua-v1-runtime-rpc-typescript/.../irt/formatter.ts` to use `import moment from 'moment'` (default import) AND flip `esModuleInterop: true` in harness tsconfig — runtime-module change with downstream-consumer impact; (b) switch harness from tsx to `tsc → node dist/`, removing the esbuild transform layer; (c) accept the drop for now. PR-03.3a chose (c). 16 of 17 wireIds and 19 fixtures land. PR-03.4 should re-evaluate when Layer C cross-language coverage matters more. AllTypes wire-format primitives are partially covered by other fixtures (Point: TInt/TString/TBool; UserId: TUUID; etc.), but TUInt64/TInt64-boundary/TFloat/TTsTz/TTsLocal/TTsUni/TTime/TDate/TList-of-self/TSet-of-self/TMap remain TS-leg gaps.

Empirical 700-file sha256 audit on the dev host (2 cold sbt-JVM invocations) shows current goldens reproducible there; cross-machine determinism not proven. If CI or downstream contributors observe `verifyGoldens` flake on a different host, extend the F6-style `groupBy(...).toSeq.sortBy(...)` fix to these sites. PR-03.1 ships without this fix per user's per-case approval boundary on F6. See `defects.md` PR-03.1-D13.
- [~] **F-TextTree — Adopt `TextTree` from izumi for renderer composition.** Baboon (sibling project from the same team) uses `izumi.fundamentals.platform.strings.TextTree` for tree-structured code emission with clean composition + indentation handling. The new idealingua-v1 translators ported the legacy raw-string-template approach faithfully to maintain byte-parity during the port. Now that legacy is deleted, refactoring `toscala/domain/`, `totypescript/domain/`, `tocsharp/domain/` renderers to compose via `TextTree` instead of raw string templates + `IzString` helpers would: (a) reduce template-as-DSL fragility, (b) match baboon's idioms for forward maintenance, (c) make multi-line indentation correct-by-construction. Substantial refactor; refined LOC estimate: ~9,700 LOC across `toscala/domain/`, `totypescript/domain/`, `tocsharp/domain/` (previous "~3000 LOC" was low by ~3x). Not on the M2 release critical path.
  - **M1 (SUPERSEDED 2026-05-12)** — `DomainTSAliasRenderer` ported as foundation cycle, but chose the wrong renderer protocol: `TextTree[Nothing]` + `.render`, which discarded the type-parameter's value entirely and prevented the layouter from post-processing for imports. User correction (2026-05-12): the type parameter's intended purpose is to let transpilers reconcile type references *after* the tree is composed (collect them, then `.mapRender` to render with correct imports + names). See M1.5 below.
  - **M1.5 (DONE 2026-05-12)** — architecture correction: `DomainTSAliasRenderer` rewritten to emit `TextTree[TSRefHandle]` and render via `.mapRender(resolver.resolve)` at the `AliasProduct.content: String` boundary. New supporting types: `TSRefHandle` (sealed ADT, currently one case `TypeRef(typeId: TypeId)`) under `totypescript/domain/TSRefHandle.scala`; `DomainTSTypeResolver` (wraps `DomainTSTypeConverter.toNativeType` for in-tree substitution, plus `harvestTypeIds(refs)` for the upstream import-collection pathway) under `totypescript/domain/DomainTSTypeResolver.scala`. Pattern source: baboon-compiler's `TsValue` + `TsBaboonTranslator.renderTree` (`/nix/store/4pd0h90s2b7ns50wbxbn8zvn5ps9cbxl-source/baboon-compiler/src/main/scala/io/septimalmind/baboon/translator/typescript/`) which `o.tree.values.collect`s `TsType` refs into the import block then `full.mapRender { ... }` substitutes per-ref names. Renderer protocol relocked at **option B''-lite**: products still carry `content: String`, but the renderer composes typed `TextTree[TSRefHandle]` internally and calls `.mapRender` at the boundary, so the harvest pathway is exercised end-to-end. Full layouter-driven import-section integration (option B') is deferred until enough renderers carry typed trees that the per-file `.values` projection is meaningful — that work is M2's scope. Alias renderer LOC: 90 → 105 (mostly protocol documentation). 8/8 verification matrix (`verifyGoldens` + `runWireFixtures` 26/20/21 + `runCrossLangInterop` 118/10/6 + harness 42/42, on both Scala 2.13.18 and 3.8.3) — green.
  - **M2 (DONE 2026-05-12)** — remaining `totypescript/domain/` renderers + the two extensions ported under the M1.5 protocol (emit `TextTree[TSRefHandle]`, render via `.mapRender(resolver.resolve)`): `DomainTSEnumRenderer`, `DomainTSIdRenderer`, `DomainTSInterfaceRenderer`, `DomainTSCompositeRenderer`, `DomainTSAdtRenderer`, `DomainTSServiceMethodProduct`, `DomainTSServiceRenderer`, plus `DomainTSEnumHelpersExtension` and `DomainTSIntrospectionExtension`. Witness ADT extended with `TSRefHandle.SerializedTypeRef(typeId)` (resolver delegates to `conv.toNativeType(id, forSerialized = true)`) so the `forSerialized` flow harvests too. **TextTree.stripMargin pitfall discovered + worked around**: `TextTree.stripMargin` strips margin per-`StringNode` child of the outer `Node`. When the `q"..."` interpolator splits a logical text line across an interpolation boundary, a literal `|` adjacent to the interpolation (e.g. `data?: $eid| ${eid}Serialized` in the Interface companion's polymorphic-section type-union) lands in its own `StringNode("| ")`, where line-start = `|`, and gets stripped. Fixed by precomposing the type-union substring into a single `String` (`${eid + "| " + eid + "Serialized"}`) so the `|` is non-line-leading. Option B''-lite stays as the renderer protocol: products keep `content: String`; `.mapRender(resolver.resolve)` is called at each product boundary. Option B' (single `.mapRender` per output file with union-of-`.values` import section) **did not materialize** — `DomainTSImports` still owns import computation independently of the tree harvest; lifting it would require threading `TextTree[TSRefHandle]` through every product type (`AliasProduct`, `IdentifierProduct`, `CompositeProduct`, `InterfaceProduct`, `AdtProduct`, `ServiceProduct`, `BuzzerProduct`) and rewriting `DomainTypeScriptTranslator`'s module assembly, which is a separate evolution beyond M2 scope.
  - **F-Scala-TextTree (follow-up, OUT OF SCOPE this cycle)** — Scala translator migration: the user has flagged that `scala.meta.quasiquotes` (currently used by `toscala/domain/`) has "annoying downsides" and the Scala translator should be migrated to the same `TextTree[TRefHandle]` protocol. Per-language `TRefHandle` ADT + resolver (Scala-side: `<package>.<typeName>` with `import` management). Tracked separately so the TS port can land without entangling the Scala surface.
  - **F-CS-TextTree (follow-up)** — C# translator migration (~12 files). Per-language `CSRefHandle` ADT + resolver (`<namespace>.<typeName>` with `using` import management). Same protocol as TS once M2 stabilises.
- [x] **F6 — Legacy `ScalaTranslator` HashMap-iteration non-determinism (surfaced during PR-03.1 T2/T3, resolved 2026-05-04 by user).** `ScalaTranslator.translate()` at `idealingua-v1-transpilers/src/main/scala/.../toscala/ScalaTranslator.scala:33-38` collected aliases into `.toMultimap` (immutable HashMap) then iterated — emitted package-object.scala alias lines in a JVM-instance-dependent order. Empirically reproduced: two cold `sbt regenerateGoldens` runs produced different alias orderings on `idltest/aliases`, `idltest/aliases2`, `izumi/test/domain01..03`. T2/T3 executor applied a 13-line fix (sort by `ModuleId.toString` after `groupBy`) — verified deterministic across multiple sbt-JVM cold starts (700 files, identical sha256). **User decision (2026-05-04)**: keep the fix; the "purely additive" constraint from `job.md` is relaxed for this minimal architecturally-justified change. PR-03.1 commits the fix alongside the harness scaffold and goldens.
- [x] **F5 — TBLOB Q3 divergence (resolved 2026-05-12).** Original status: all three transpilers diverged from Q3 (locked as base64-string for all three). Scala mapped TBLOB → `Array[Byte]` with Circe's default int-array encoder; TS emitted `???` placeholders that threw at codegen; C# emitted `???` similarly. **Resolved** in commit `PR-02 F5-fix: TBLOB → base64 string on all three emitters (+ corpus fixture)`: (a) **Scala** — TBLOB still maps to `Array[Byte]`, but `IRTTimeInstances` now provides implicit `Encoder[Array[Byte]]` / `Decoder[Array[Byte]]` using `java.util.Base64` standard alphabet + padding. Every generated Circe trait extends `IRTTimeInstances` so the override is in scope for `deriveEncoder`/`deriveDecoder`. `ScalaTypeConverter.toScala(id: TypeId)` adds a `Primitive.TBLOB` case that routes through the private generic-style path to produce `Array[Byte]` correctly (the prior `toScala(JavaType, parameters)` path was dead — it would have thrown). (b) **TypeScript** — TBLOB → `string` (base64 already at the TS layer). All `???` sites in `DomainTSTypeConverter` collapse to string-passthrough (mirrors `TUUID` shape); field getter/setter uses `toStringField` (no GUID-format validation). `DomainTSIntrospectionExtension` emits `{intro: IntrospectorTypes.Blob}`; `Blob` added as the last enum member to `idealingua-v1-runtime-rpc-typescript/.../irt/introspector.ts`. (c) **C#** — TBLOB → `byte[]`. `DomainCSharpType.renderPrimitiveType` returns `"byte[]"`; `isNullable=true`; `getRandomValue` produces a `new byte[] { ... }` literal; `renderToString`/`renderFromString` use `System.Convert.ToBase64String`/`FromBase64String`. `DomainCSJsonNetExtension.writePropertyValue` emits `writer.WriteValue(System.Convert.ToBase64String($src));`; reader emits `System.Convert.FromBase64String($src.Value<string>())`. `propertyNeedsPrepare=false` (scalar single-value write). `DomainCSImports` imports `System` for TBLOB and treats it as nullable. **Corpus fixture**: `idltest/blobtest.domain` declares `data BlobHolder { payload: blb; label: str }`; wire-fixture `with-bytes.json` in each language's tree contains `{"payload":"aGkh","label":"greeting"}` (3 bytes "hi!" → base64 "aGkh"). `WireDispatch` (Scala), `Dispatch.ts`, `Dispatch.cs` each register the new wireId. **Verification matrix (4 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 8 lines all green)**: Scala 2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each — `verifyGoldens` ok (new BlobHolder.scala/BlobHolder.ts/BlobHolder.cs goldens regenerated), `runWireFixtures` 26 Scala + 20 TypeScript + 21 CSharp, `runCrossLangInterop` 118 verified / 10 skipped / 6 excluded (new `idltest.blobtest.BlobHolder` row contributes +6 verified across all language pairs), `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 42/42. F2 closes alongside.

Grammar surface to remove enumerated in PR-02 §9 C7: keyword `Keywords.scala:25`, parser entry `DefStructure.scala:131-136`, raw AST nodes `RawTypeDef.scala:32` and `RawTopLevelDefn.scala:24`. §12 Q4 marked RESOLVED.

## Completed

- **PR-02 F-TextTree M7: port Scala service renderers off scala.meta** (2026-05-12) — Seventh milestone of the F-TextTree multi-cycle rewrite; fourth cycle on the Scala renderer family. Migrates `DomainServiceRenderer` and `DomainServiceMethodProduct` from `scala.meta` quasiquotes onto `TextTree[ScalaRefHandle]` composition + `.mapRender(resolver.resolve)` at the renderer boundary. **Renderers ported** (LOC before → after): `DomainServiceRenderer.scala` 129 → 189; `DomainServiceMethodProduct.scala` 447 → 542. `DomainServiceContext.scala` unchanged at 82 (it carries small `scala.meta` Name/Type witnesses — `F.t`, `Ctx.t`, `Ctx.p`, `IO2.n`, `methodImport` — that the renderer lowers via `DomainScalaParseBack.renderS30(_)` at splice points; migrating those is M8 scope and would cascade into the small `Term.Name`/`Type.Name`/`tparam` API). **No foundation extensions required** — the witnesses M6 introduced (`TypeName`, `TypeFull`, `TermFull`, `TypeAbsolute`, `TypeFullWithin`, `TermFullWithin`) cover every service-renderer substitution shape. For `<svcBase>.<Output>.{Success,Failure}` paths the renderer composes `ScalaType.within(_).typeFull.toString` directly rather than introducing a 2-deep `TypeFullWithinWithin` witness — keeps the resolver dispatch minimal. **`DomainScalaParseBack` extensions**: none (already-`Defn` material flows through `renderS30(_)` for spliced fragments; the methods-object skeleton parses back via `parseObject`, and the per-method I/O DTOs already arrive as a `List[Defn]` from the M6 composite renderer + extension chain). **Carrier strategy** (same as M5/M6): `CogenServiceProduct` carrier preserved — 6 `Defn` slots (server / client / clientWrapped / serverWrapped / methods / marshallers) plus an `imports: List[Import]` preamble. Each top-level Defn is composed as a `TextTree`, rendered to `String` via `.mapRender(resolver.resolve)`, then parse-back via `DomainScalaParseBack.{parseTrait, parseClass, parseObject}` at the renderer boundary. The methods-object splices `decls.flatMap(_.defStructs)` as already-built `Defn` list via `scalaMetaTools.appendDefinitions(_)` on the parsed skeleton — this avoids a text round-trip for the I/O DTO surface (which is heavy: Circe trait + companion-base + AnyVal mixin + CastSimilar / CastUp implicit objects, all built and parsed once by the composite renderer's `withCirce` path). **`DomainServiceMethodProduct` public API change**: per-method helpers (`defnServer`, `defnClient`, `defnServerWrapped`, `defnClientWrapped`, `defnMethod`, `defnCodec`, `defnMethodRegistration`, `defnCodecRegistration`) now return `TextTree[ScalaRefHandle]` instead of `Defn`. `defStructs: List[Defn]` is the only `Defn`-returning member retained (consumed by the methods-object splice). The sole external caller is `DomainServiceRenderer.renderImpl` which threads everything through TextTree composition; no other call site touched. **New pitfalls surfaced** (all fixed inline): (1) **Scala 3 indentation-sensitive parsing inside braces** — even with brace-delimited blocks, scalameta's Scala 3 parser uses source indentation as a parsing hint when a stat (`case` arm, `def` body, etc.) inside a `{ … }` block has an indent flatter than the brace's opening column. Symptom: codec defs' partial-function `case` arms at source col 2 inside an outer object at col 2 parsed but then the printer emitted the case arm at col-0 rather than at brace-nested depth. Fix: every `${nestedTree}` splice point that introduces deeper nesting must apply `.shift(N).trim` so the parser sees properly-indented source. Applied at: per-method `${invoke.shift(2).trim}` in `defnServerWrapped`, all four `${defnEncoder/Decoder.shift(2).trim}` in `defnCodec`, and the existing `${exception.shift(N).trim}` sites in `defnClientWrapped`. (2) **`StringNode` escape-processing on pre-rendered Defn text** — `TextTree.q"…$str…"` builds a `StringNode(str)` for the string interpolation arg; rendering applies `StringContext.processEscapes`, which collapses `\\"` to `"`. Pre-rendered ADT Circe text from `withAdtCirce` (the service-output ADT path) contains literal `\"` in `DecodingFailure("...{ \"type_name\": ...}")` — the splice strips the escapes and produces ungrammatical source. Fix: splice via `TextTree.verbatim(_)` for pre-rendered Scala text containing `\"` / `\\n` etc. — `VerbatimNode` skips `processEscapes`. M5/M6 did not surface this because their renderers don't splice already-rendered Defn text containing escapes; the service path is the first to route ADT codec source through TextTree. (3) **scalameta printer's mixed-Defn-shape indent quirk** — when an object body interleaves a short single-line `final case class X() extends Y` (no body) followed by a `trait Z {…}`, the Scala 3 printer emits the trait at column 0 rather than the object's body indent. Surface area: the methods-object's `defStructs` splice mixes case classes and traits per-method. Fix: parse the methods object skeleton WITHOUT defStructs, then `appendDefinitions(_)` the pre-built Defn list — this skips the text round-trip and uses scalameta's tree-level append which produces correct output. (4) **Trailing-comma-on-empty-args pitfall** — `def $name(ctx: $ctxT, ${signatureText})` produces `def f(ctx: C, )` when signatureText is empty (zero-arg server methods). Fix: introduced `signaturePrefix` / `sigCallPrefix` helpers that emit `", " + s` when non-empty, empty otherwise — applied to the four trait/wrapped server/client method-decl sites. (5) **String-interpolation arg-as-Block printer canonicalization** — `s"... ${v.getClass}"` source-form vs scalameta's `Term.Interpolate(s, parts, args)` AST: when the arg is a single `Term.Select` (e.g. `v.getClass`), the printer emits multi-line `s"... ${\n  v.getClass\n}"` for visual clarity (legacy q-built tree). Reproducing this required emitting the `${…}` arg literally inside a multi-line block in the TextTree source so parse-back builds the same shape. **Verification matrix (4 FROZEN × 2 Scala = 8 lines all green)**: Scala 2.13.18 + 3.8.3 each — `verifyGoldens` byte-equal (all service goldens — `TestService`, `TestBuzzer`, `OptionalService`, `ImportIdService`, `NestedAdtsService`, `TestAliasServ` — match across both dialects), `runWireFixtures` 26 Scala + 20 TypeScript + 21 CSharp, `runCrossLangInterop` 118 verified / 10 skipped / 6 excluded, `idealingua-v1-test-harness/test` 42/42. **`DomainSTContext`, `DomainScalaTranslator`, `ModuleTools.toSource` all unchanged**. **Surface area remaining for F-TextTree** (M8): extensions — `DomainCirceTranslatorExtensionBase` 504, `DomainCastSimilarExtension` 162, `DomainCastUpExtension` 273, `DomainCastDownExpandExtension` 127, `DomainAnyvalExtension` 152 — total ~1218 LOC. Once extensions emit `String` directly the carrier (`CogenProduct[Defn.{Class, Trait}]`, `EnumProduct`, `AdtProduct`, `CogenServiceProduct`) can flip String-everywhere and `DomainScalaParseBack` retires, completing scala.meta removal from the Scala translator family. Supporting scaffolding (`DomainScalaStruct`, `DomainCompositeStructure`, `DomainServiceContext`'s `F`/`Ctx`/`IO2` Name witnesses) migrate alongside the extensions since they share the `Defn`-shaped product surface.

- **PR-02 F-TextTree M6: port Scala identifier + DTO + interface renderers off scala.meta** (2026-05-13) — Sixth milestone of the F-TextTree multi-cycle rewrite; third cycle on the Scala renderer family. Migrates `DomainIdRenderer`, `DomainCompositeRenderer`, and `DomainInterfaceRenderer` from `scala.meta` quasiquotes onto `TextTree[ScalaRefHandle]` composition + `.mapRender(resolver.resolve)` at the renderer boundary. **Renderers ported** (LOC before → after): `DomainIdRenderer.scala` 125 → 180; `DomainCompositeRenderer.scala` 122 → 174; `DomainInterfaceRenderer.scala` 192 → 241 (growth across all three is renderer-protocol documentation + per-witness substitution sites + the supporting-scaffolding splice points). **Foundation extended**: `ScalaRefHandle.scala` 77 → 88 LOC — added one witness case: `TermFull(typeId: TypeId)` (mirror of `TypeFull` for term-position fully-qualified references, used at `${type.termFull}.parse(...)` parser call sites in `DomainIdRenderer`). `DomainScalaTextResolver.scala` 57 → 59 LOC — resolver + `harvestTypeIds` updated to dispatch on the new case. **Boundary helper extended**: `DomainScalaParseBack.scala` 56 → 74 LOC — added `renderS30[T <: Tree](tree: T): String = dialect(tree).syntax` for scaffolding-fragment splicing under the Scala 3 dialect. Surfaced as a defect in round 1: the plain `Tree.syntax` extension uses the default scalameta dialect (Scala 2.13), which does NOT backtick-escape Scala 3 reserved words used as identifiers (`export` was the trigger — `Decl.Def(_, Term.Name("export"), _, Type.Name("Boolean")).syntax` emits `def export: Boolean`, which the Scala 3 parse-back parser correctly rejects). `renderS30` forces dialect-aware printing so `Term.Name("export").syntax` yields `` `export` ``, keyword-safe at parse-back. All `Tree → String` splice sites in the three renderers route through `renderS30`. **Carrier strategy** (same as M5): parse-back-at-boundary chosen. Each renderer's `CogenProduct[Defn.{Class,Trait}]` carrier is preserved — downstream extensions (Circe sibling + base, AnyVal `prependBase`, CastSimilar / CastUp / CastDownExpand `appendDefinitions`) continue to mutate the `Defn` surface unchanged. **Supporting files NOT ported**: `DomainScalaStruct.scala` (233 LOC) and `DomainCompositeStructure.scala` (193 LOC) emit `scala.meta` fragments (`Term.Param`, `Defn.Def`, `Init`) as scaffolding consumed by both renderers AND extensions. Threading `TextTree` through them would cascade into the extension layer (M8 scope). Their `Tree` fragments are spliced into the renderers' `TextTree` envelopes via `renderS30(_)` text. **`DomainInterfaceRenderer.mkTrait` preserved as `Defn.Trait`-returning** because external callers (`DomainCompositeRenderer.defns` mirror-interface synthesis, `AnyvalExtension.handleTrait` via `TraitProduct(qqInterface).defn.prependBase`) consume it as a `Defn`. **Byte-parity pitfalls surfaced** (all fixed inline): (1) **Empty-brace pitfall (M5-class)** — the legacy `q"final case class … {}".syntax` printer drops `{}` but the parser preserves source-level braces. The renderer therefore emits `final case class Foo(...) extends ...` (no trailing braces) and `implicit class FooExtensions(...) extends ...` (no trailing braces) for byte parity. `mkTrait` emits `trait T extends X { decls }` only when decls is non-empty; otherwise `trait T extends X`. (2) **Scala 3 keyword-as-identifier escape** — described above (`renderS30`). (3) **Identifier toString sort-order** — the legacy `parts = sortedFields.map(...)` iterates the alpha-sorted field list (same as the parsers' iteration), NOT declaration order. The prior in-source comment in the legacy renderer mislabelled this as "declaration order"; golden output (`Seq(this.company, this.value)` for declaration order `value, company`) confirms sort order. The M6 renderer iterates `sortedFields` for `partsBuilders`. **`TextTree` + `scala.meta` quasiquote conflict** surfaced as a compile error: both libraries export `q"..."` interpolators. Resolution: import `izumi.fundamentals.platform.strings.TextTree.*` for the TextTree `q`, and selectively import scala.meta members by name (e.g. `import scala.meta.Term`, plus `import scala.meta.{Decl, Defn, Init}` in `DomainInterfaceRenderer`) rather than the wildcard `import scala.meta.*` (which would shadow `q`).
